Wait, no:\n\nWait, perhaps doing this step-by-step:\n\nAfter expansion:\n\nSum from n=0 to 30 of [1 term: z^n]\n\nMinus sum from n=0 to 30 of [2z term: z^{n+1}]\n\nPlus sum from n=0 to 30 of [z^2 term: z^{n+2}]\n\nSo:\n\n= sum_{n=0}^{30} z^n - 2 sum_{n=0}^{30} z^{n+1} + sum_{n=0}^{30} z^{n+2}\n\n= sum_{n=0}^{30} z^n - 2 sum_{n=1}^{31} z^n + sum_{n=2}^{32} z^n\n\nCombine the sums:\n\n= [1 + z + z^2 + ... + z^{30}] - 2 [z + z^2 + ... + z^{31}] + [z^2 + z^3 + ... + z^{32}]\n\nNow, convert each term into sum notation:\n\nFirst term: sum_{n=0}^{30} z^n\n\nSecond term: -2 sum_{n=1}^{31} z^n\n\nThird term: sum_{n=2}^{32} z^n\n\nCombine all terms:\n\nFor n=0: coefficient 1\n\nFor n=1: 0 -2( from second term) = -2\n\nFor n=2: 1 (from first term) -2 (from second term) +1 (from third term) = 0\n\nFor n=3 to n=30: Each has coefficient 1 (from first term) -2 (from second term) +1 (from third term) = 0\n\nFor n=31: From the first term, coefficient 0, second term has -2, third term 0. Total: -2\n\nFor n=32: From third term, coefficient 1; first and second terms 0. Total:1\n\nThus, the expansion is:\n\n1 - 2z -2z^{31} + z^{32}\n\nWhich is not matching the required polynomial. Hence, this is also not the correct approach.\n\nGiven that these trial and error methods aren\'t yielding results, perhaps think of the problem differently.
